# Python binding implementation plan

Status: implementation roadmap; reviewed against the current checkout on 2026-10-11.
Scope: finish the existing embedded Python implementation and ship it across Linux,
Windows, and macOS. This plan does not require rewriting the bindings.
Maintenance: this is a change-triggered, self-updating implementation record.
Contributors reconcile implementation, test, CI, design, and user-scope changes
here in the same change; the read-only checker validates structure, complete
candidate-path coverage, and receipt synchronization after each update. Locally,
`scripts/validate_python_binding_plan.py --worktree` inventories staged,
unstaged, and non-ignored untracked paths; use explicit exclusions only for
generated output trees and record them in the receipt. The checker does not
author semantic updates or promote gates. Use the ongoing loop in section 6;
synchronization is a required gate, not a final documentation pass. After each
plan edit, run `--full`, the checker regression suite, whitespace checks, and
complete-candidate `--worktree`; at PR handoff, also check the tracked diff with
`--base-ref <actual-base>`. Record each result or blocker in the newest receipt.
These checks prove plan synchronization, not product behavior.

## 1. Completion contract and platform matrix

The same supported scripting capabilities must work in source builds and installed
artifacts: kernel/builders, document operations and undo/redo, events, UI callbacks,
custom entities, persistence, ScriptDock, and bundled plugins. Public modules remain
`lc`, `lcgui` (UI builds), and `lcUIPy`. Feature completeness is measured by behavior,
not by exposing every C++ method or porting every Lua tool to Python.

The primary workflow builds Linux and Windows artifacts, and a separate native
macOS arm64/x86_64 workflow has been added. The macOS job has passed local
arm64 source/install/relocation checks but has not yet run on hosted runners;
hosted macOS results remain an automation-validation gap, not evidence that
macOS should be excluded. macOS source builds and bundles are required
deliverables in this roadmap.

| Platform / distribution | Architecture scope | Runtime policy | Required proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| Linux system/developer install | x86_64; native arm64 build check | System Python SDK and declared runtime dependency | Installed-prefix imports and core tests |
| Linux AppImage | x86_64 initially, matching existing deploy tools | Bundle the Python selected at build time | Actual AppImage smoke in a clean runtime environment |
| Linux Snap | amd64 initially; declare it explicitly | Build SDK and staged runtime from the same base archive | Installed snap smoke; inspect staged library closure |
| Windows NSIS | x64, matching existing Qt/C++ lane | Matching official CPython SDK/runtime staged from the selected install | Installed EXE smoke and DLL/extension loading |
| macOS app bundle | Separate arm64 and x86_64 artifacts | Matching prebuilt Python framework/runtime per architecture | Relocated .app smoke, dependency and signing checks |

Do not imply ARM AppImage/Snap, Windows ARM/32-bit, or universal2 support without
their own artifact lane. Keep packaging paths architecture-aware so those lanes
can be added without binding changes. Record each artifact's OS floor from the
maximum requirement of LibreCAD, Qt, Python, and its native dependencies. An
AppImage built on the current Ubuntu runner does not automatically support older
glibc systems; build on the declared oldest baseline if that compatibility is promised.

There is currently no Debian/RPM release pipeline. Improve the normal CMake install
and document distro dependencies; creating new native package formats is outside
this change.

## 2. Compact design decisions

- Retain one interpreter per process, the existing permanent GIL-release policy,
  and process-lifetime interpreter ownership. Initialize on the application thread.
- Retain shared `lcpythonscript` and `lcscripting` libraries. Register kernel types
  only in `lc`; register `lcgui` once in the executable or test UI library.
- Match the SDK, linked runtime, stdlib, architecture, and build flavor **within
  each artifact**. Different supported CPython minors across OS distributions are
  acceptable; forcing one minor would add unnecessary dependency builds.
- Use existing distribution packages/prebuilt SDKs. Cache and record exact versions,
  download hashes where applicable, and licenses. Do not build CPython from source
  in ordinary CI.
- Keep Windows dependency resolution ABI-aligned: pin `windows-2022` for Qt's
  `win64_msvc2022_64` binaries and resolve Conan dependencies with
  `--build=never`. A missing compatible binary must fail fast instead of
  compiling that dependency from source; CI still builds LibreCAD and its
  in-tree components normally.
- Stage the provider's standard library and native modules without a hand-curated
  import subset. Document provider exclusions such as Tk/IDLE and pip on Windows;
  in-application dependency installation and multiprocessing support are separate features.
- Keep runtime configuration in the existing facade plus one generated CMake
  configuration/header and one staging helper. Reuse current adapters, dispatch
  interface, tests, and package scripts.
- Keep `ScriptValue` conversion lossless for its declared types: `None`/nil,
  primitives, string-key maps, and non-empty homogeneous Coordinate/entity
  sequences. Reject empty or mixed sequences, non-string map keys, out-of-range
  integers, and arbitrary Python objects with diagnostics; do not silently
  coerce or turn conversion failures into `Nil`.
- Keep existing Lua/native call signatures working. Add checked calls only at the
  few operation/event boundaries that need to distinguish failure from Python `None`.
- Treat plugins as user-trusted code. Isolated import configuration provides
  predictable lookup; it does not sandbox scripts.

The release implementation is complete only after every platform row passes.
Signing/notarization credentials belong to protected release jobs; PR validation
must work with unsigned or ad-hoc-signed macOS bundles.

## 4. Implementation sub-plans

### S0 — Freeze a bounded API and runtime inventory

**Files:** `doxygen/Scripting.md`, `README.md`,
`lcadpythonscript/bridge/py_lc_*.cpp`, `lcUI/python/pyguibridge.cpp`,
`conanfile.py`, package scripts.

1. Add a short capability table to the existing scripting document: namespaces,
   entities/builders, storage, operations/undo, persistence, events, UI/widgets,
   operation metadata, and custom entities. Label each as tested, incomplete, or
   deliberately unsupported; include Python collection/constructor differences.
2. Use existing Lua APIs and shipped Python plugins as the required surface.
   Fill functional gaps they exercise; leave unrelated C++ methods out of scope.
3. Capture the Python chosen by CMake: version, executable, headers/import library,
   shared runtime, stdlib, dynamic modules, architecture, and provider. At
   configure time, verify the selected interpreter's exact version and headers
   against FindPython, require target-matching pointer width, and reject
   non-CPython, debug, or free-threaded providers until their ABI/GIL models are
   explicitly validated. A Debug LibreCAD build may still use the supported
   release CPython provider; do not infer Python ABI mode from `CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE`.
4. Record versions per release artifact and baseline OS. Source builds retain
   ordinary `find_package(Python ... Interpreter Development.Embed)` discovery.

**Exit:** no undefined claim of “full parity”; a small capability table and a
reproducible runtime inventory drive the work below.

### S1 — Correct CMake feature flags, exports, and install rules

**Files:** root, `lcadpythonscript`, `lcadluascript`, `lcscripting`, `lcUI`, `lcUIPy`,
`luacmdinterface`, and `unittest/CMakeLists.txt`;
`lcadpythonscript/lcpython_api.h`.

1. Create the enabled persistence target before both language targets and set
   `USE_PERSISTENCE=0/1` explicitly on `lcpythonscript` and `lcluascript`.
   Both use the conditional; remove the late global definition after auditing
   its consumers. Avoid a repository-wide CMake cleanup.
2. When enabled, propagate `LC_WITH_PYTHONSCRIPT` through the binding target's
   usage requirements to UI, CLI, and tests. Audit every current macro consumer
   before removing the root global definition.
3. Use a library-build export definition with `dllexport` for producers and
   `dllimport` for Windows consumers; preserve hidden pybind11 implementation types.
   Fix only cross-library boundaries, not global visibility policy.
4. Add `RUNTIME DESTINATION` for Python/neutral scripting DLLs. Use relative
   `install(FILES|DIRECTORY|TARGETS ...)` destinations that honor prefix, DESTDIR,
   and CPack staging. Eliminate absolute install-time `file(WRITE)` path generation.
5. Preserve one loaded registration of each embedded module. The application
   must not also link the test-only `lcui` library that duplicates UI module code.
6. Generate `build_constants.cpp` in `CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR`, not in the
   source checkout. Keep Git metadata lookup rooted at the source tree, and
   prove a fresh out-of-tree build works without creating or rewriting source
   files; this also prevents concurrent build trees from racing on one file.

**Required checks:** Python/Lua persistence namespaces and round trips in the
Python on/persistence on application; Python off application with Python package
discovery disabled; and Python on/persistence off `lcpythonscript` target build.
Inspect generated definitions and exports. Exercise the provider guard for the
matching host, a version/header mismatch, a pointer-width mismatch, debug CPython,
and a free-threaded provider; unsupported providers must fail with a diagnostic
before application compilation. The generated build-info source must be under
the binary tree and a fresh out-of-tree `lcunittest` build must not write into
the source tree.

**Current feature-matrix result (2026-10-11):** On native macOS 27 arm64,
AppleClang 21, CPython 3.14.8, and Lua 5.5.1, a fresh Release configure with
`WITH_PYTHONSCRIPT=ON`, `WITH_PERSISTENCE=OFF`, and `WITH_QT_UI=OFF` passes the
provider validator and builds `lcpythonscript`. The resulting dylib is arm64,
links to the selected Python.framework plus `lcscripting`/`lckernel`, and has no
persistence dependency; CMake exposes no `persistence`, `lcui`, or `librecad`
target in this feature configuration. On Ubuntu 26.04 x86_64 emulated on the
local ARM64 host, the same feature flags validate CPython 3.14.4 x86_64 and
`cmake --build /tmp/librecad-python-persistence-off-x64-current --target lcpythonscript --parallel 4`
succeeds. The native-ISA Ubuntu 26.04 ARM64 container also configures those
flags with CPython 3.14.4/aarch64 and builds `lcpythonscript`; its ELF is
aarch64 and `ldd` shows Python, `lcscripting`, and `lckernel` with no persistence
library. These are local developer-target checks: Linux x86_64 is emulated, and
neither Linux result proves hosted or package acceptance.

**Scope constraint:** the current UI links persistence unconditionally. All release
artifacts retain persistence ON. Do not promise a full persistence-OFF UI or add
that unrelated refactor to this plan; the OFF check targets the binding library.

### S2 — Finish typed callbacks and API behavior

**Files:** `lcscripting/customentitydispatchhook.{h,cpp}`,
`lcscripting/primitive/customentity.cpp`,
`lcadpythonscript/scriptadapter/`,
`lcadluascript/scriptadapter/customentitydispatch_lua.cpp`,
`lcUI/python/pyguibridge.cpp`, `lcUIPy/plugins/rectangle/plugin.py`,
`lcUI/lcadviewer.cpp`,
`lckernel/cad/storage/undomanagerimpl.cpp`,
`unittest/CMakeLists.txt`, `unittest/lckernel/operations/buildertest.cpp`.

1. Reuse the existing six-method custom-entity dispatch interface. Select a hook
   by callback runtime (`lua` / `python`) in a small keyed table; installation
   must be idempotent and must not overwrite the other language's hook.
2. Add the Python hook using the already-bound `Insert`, `EntityCoordinate`,
   `SimpleSnapConstrain`, and `operation.Builder` types. Return real snap vectors
   and integer-keyed drag maps; pass the real transaction builder to click/release.
   A handled callback failure must report an error and use a safe empty/default
   result, without falling through and invoking the callback a second time.
3. Remove the rectangle plugin's direct-execution drag fallback once builder
   context works. Preserve one undo transaction across click/drag/release;
   when the same mutable Builder is committed at press and release, keep one
   undo entry that covers its complete operation list.
4. Share private Python value conversion between callback/object adapters
   within `lcpythonscript`. Complete supported list/map conversions and report
   unsupported or out-of-range values instead of silently returning nil.
5. Add one UI-side materialization seam for the opaque tags actually used by
   callbacks (`CadMdiChild` and menus). Keep Qt dependencies out of
   `lcscripting` and `lcadpythonscript`; reject unknown tags diagnostically.
6. Review holder/return policies: Qt parent ownership stays in C++; kernel
   shared-pointer identity survives round trips; entity mutation stays on
   supported builder/operation APIs. Use guarded access for retained UI handles.
7. Define `KAGUYA_SUPPORT_MULTIPLE_SHARED_LIBRARY=1` for every build target.
   Lua types are registered in one shared library and used from others; without
   Kaguya's cross-module registry, value types such as `geo::Coordinate` can
   arrive in callbacks as `UnknownType` on non-MSVC builds too.

**Tests:** all six custom-entity slots with actual typed arguments/results; snap
cleanup; stable point IDs; re-executing one Builder remains one undo/redo
transaction; Lua/Python hooks coexist in either initialization order; supported
UI payloads are usable; Lua `Insert` and `Coordinate` values cross shared-library
boundaries with usable metatables; wrong types fail clearly; references survive
or invalidate according to ownership. MainWindow/ScriptDock construction and
`autoScale()` before a current OpenGL context must be safe; defer painter creation
until `initializeGL` and autoscaling until a painter exists. This keeps Qt binding
tests and normal document setup from calling GL without a context. The shipped
rectangle plugin must not broadly suppress failures from its guaranteed bound
`setMetaInfo` and `TempEntities` calls; a real GUI regression verifies drag
preview additions/removals trigger canvas refresh events while a real
`MainWindowExecutionScope` is active, matching host callback dispatch.

### S3 — Make interpreter and installed paths deterministic

**Files:** `lcadpythonscript/lcpython.{h,cpp}`, `lcUI/main.cpp`,
`luacmdinterface/main.cpp`, `lcUIPy/CMakeLists.txt`,
`lcUI/luainterface.cpp`, `lcUI/python/pypluginloader.{h,cpp}`;
small proposed `cmake/PythonRuntime.cmake` and generated configuration header.

1. Supply a plain runtime configuration to the existing facade: executable path,
   runtime home, stdlib/extension paths, scripts parent, and plugin directory.
   Resolve release paths from the executable/bundle; use explicit source paths
   only for developer/test configurations. No recursive installed-tree searches.
2. In the GUI, configure after `QApplication` exists and before
   `WindowManager::init()`. Configure the CLI and test entry points too.
   Retain a no-argument system/developer initializer for existing kernel tests;
   reject a conflicting configuration after the first initialization.
3. For packaged builds use `PyConfig_InitIsolatedConfig`, checked path setters,
   `parse_argv=0`, `site_import=0`, `use_environment=0`,
   `install_signal_handlers=0`, `write_bytecode=0`, UTF-8 mode selected through
   `PyPreConfig`, and explicit `module_search_paths_set=1`. Account for Unix
   stdlib/`lib-dynload` and Windows stdlib ZIP/extension directories.
4. The vendored pybind11 already supports
   `scoped_interpreter(PyConfig*, ..., add_program_dir_to_path=false)`.
   Use it so pybind11 does not reinsert the executable directory/current directory.
   Check every initialization status and surface a readable startup failure.
5. Add only the `lcUIPy` parent to imports. Pass the plugin directory directly
   to the existing file-based plugin loader; its directory need not be on
   `sys.path`. Replace dependence on `__main__.plugin_path`.
6. Remove installed dependence on `eval_file(path.py)`; retain a short
   compatibility shim only if a documented consumer needs it.
   Source/developer imports continue to work through the same configuration.
   Separate process plugin-hook installation from window event hooks so plugin
   registration is available before loading definitions, including smoke mode.
7. Catch startup exceptions at the application boundary; return nonzero for
   CLI/smoke failures. Fix `main` to return `main2`'s result so CI sees failures.

**Tests:** initialization once; repeat compatible initialization; conflicting
configuration rejected; imports from a relocated prefix containing spaces and
Unicode; missing runtime emits a useful error; poisoned host Python paths are
ignored in packaged mode. For a staged package, assert `sys.prefix` equals the
app-relative staged runtime root, not merely that imports sit under whichever
prefix Python selected. The installed-runtime smoke must also verify that every
file-backed imported standard-library and extension module resolves under that
selected `sys.prefix`, including `ctypes`, `encodings`, `gzip`, `hashlib`,
`json`, `os`, `pathlib`, `sqlite3`, `ssl`, `zlib`, and their representative
native modules. Accept modules without a file only when their spec identifies
them as built-in or frozen; do not treat an arbitrary missing origin as valid.
Initialization failure tests run as separate processes.

Native library loading is a separate requirement: the OS must find libpython and
binding libraries before this initialization code executes. S6 handles that closure.

### S4 — Bind execution to the owning window and make errors recoverable

**Files:** `lcUI/python/pyeventhooks.{h,cpp}`, `lcUI/mainwindow.cpp`,
`lcUI/windowmanager.cpp`, `lcUI/cadmdichild.cpp`,
`lcUI/luainterface.cpp`, `lcUI/widgets/scriptdock.cpp`,
`lcUI/operationloader.cpp`, `lcUIPy/create_operations.py`,
`lcscripting/{scriptcallback.h,scriptobject.h,eventbus.h,eventbus.cpp}`,
Python adapters.

1. Add a small Qt-side execution scope carrying `QPointer<MainWindow>`.
   Enter it during window script setup, ScriptDock execution, operation
   construction/init, document-open dispatch, and callbacks. Nesting restores the
   previous scope. Include the open-file path that currently runs before the new
   window is inserted into WindowManager. Custom-entity geometry/reconstruction
   callbacks use the entity document/builder; they cannot infer a UI owner from a
   process-global plugin or create one when no UI scope exists.
2. Resolve event registration and `lcgui.currentMainWindow()` from that scope;
   return `None` when there is no owner. A callback proxy stores its registering
   window and re-enters the scope on invocation. Deregistration uses that same
   owner and preserves bound-method equality.
3. Track operation-owned subscriptions together. Closing/canceling/failing an
   operation removes all its events, previews, and CLI/operation state.
   Window teardown clears its EventBus without clearing process-global plugins.
   QPointer guarding a proxy does not make an arbitrary retained raw Python
   QWidget pointer safe; test and guard the supported retained-handle APIs.
4. Load plugin definitions and global custom-entity registrations once per
   process; wire operation commands/menus once per window from the shared
   registry. Process-global plugin callbacks must not capture the first window.
   Keep per-window UI actions at operation/wiring time; identify and adapt any
   existing plugin that currently performs them during global file evaluation.
   Bootstrap order is: initialize interpreter, install process registration hooks,
   load context-free definitions once, then create windows with scoped event
   hooks and UI wiring. Global plugin loading cannot wait for a window's hook setup.
5. Give internal EventBus entries a monotonic registration ID and expose a small
   tracked-registration path. Preserve equality-based public deregistration for
   compatibility; failure cleanup removes exact IDs. Carry IDs in snapshots,
   including LuaInterface's raw-Lua dispatch path, so duplicate equal callbacks
   cannot cause the wrong subscription to be removed.
6. Add one diagnostic sink carrying plain context/traceback strings. Format
   Python errors under the GIL; queue UI presentation without retaining Python
   exceptions or acquiring Qt/UI locks from a background callback.
7. Add checked invoke/instantiate/method/attribute-write variants only at
   dispatch/operation boundaries, retaining existing wrappers. Legitimate
   `None` and absent optional attributes remain valid; constructor, conversion,
   method, and attribute-setter exceptions are failures. Catch conversion and
   `std::exception` failures as well as Python exceptions before they cross a Qt
   callback boundary. Reuse existing runString/runFile traceback returns instead
   of creating a second reporting path. Report once.
8. Disable a failing standalone listener; cancel all subscriptions of a failed
   operation. On operation close, report a callback failure once but complete
   exact subscription cleanup regardless. Perform removal after snapshot
   dispatch with no callback invocation under the EventBus mutex. Continue
   delivering to unrelated listeners.
9. Keep UI scripting on the Qt thread and enforce that at the UI bridge boundary.
   The GIL does not authorize QWidget access from Python worker threads.

**Tests:** first-window startup registers correctly before vector insertion;
A/B constructor and callback routing; ScriptDock ownership; nested/reentrant
callbacks; nested execution scopes restore the previous owner; destruction of a
window invalidates its scoped `QPointer`; fresh bound-method deregistration;
exact duplicate-registration cleanup; window close invalidation;
document-open/reconstruction ownership; plugin registration count stable across
two windows; constructor/event/property-setter/close failure cleanup and one
close diagnostic; unsupported attribute conversions return diagnostics without
mutating the object; unrelated listeners continue; UI access from a worker
fails clearly.

### S5 — Close the persistence and parity gates

**Files:** `unittest/python/{persistence_test,parity_test,demoscript_test}.cpp`,
`unittest/scripting/customentitymanager_test.cpp`, persistence adapters,
dependency pins/recipes used by Linux, Snap, Windows, and macOS.

1. Require the ordinary Python DXF save/open test after S1.
2. On the current base, validate the repository-pinned
   `third_party/libdxfrw` revision `423cf99fd26bc9938dc259907e2889666d672d1f`.
   Its `DRW_Entity` parser dispatches group 102 into bounded `parseDxfGroups`.
   Latest-base macOS arm64 Python custom-entity reconstruction and Lua/Python
   parity tests pass on this provider. Every platform workflow must use this
   recursively checked-out target; do not clone or build the older external
   `f7debf4` revision, which CMake no longer links. Run the same DXF and parity
   regressions on Linux, Windows, and Snap. If a lane fails, make a reviewed
   delta against the exact submodule revision and test that change there. Do
   not retain the superseded queued-block lookup hypothesis as a current
   diagnosis.
3. Add a mixed-language persistence regression: write a custom-entity DXF from
   Lua through the public storage/builder/File APIs, destroy the Lua state, then
   open it in a fresh document with only the Python reconstruction plugin
   registered. Assert one Python callback, concrete Insert/CustomEntityStorage
   types, plugin/entity names, parameters, and position; prove the replacement
   retains the loaded entity ID and its Python snap/drag callbacks still return
   typed values after reload; keep the document at exactly one entity. No stale
   Lua callback may participate.
4. Keep this test in the required, unskipped filter on every platform. Its
   acceptance includes parameters, loaded-ID preservation, live callback
   reconstruction, snap/drag/set-drag dispatch, and duplicate prevention after
   reload. Do not weaken assertions or serialize Python functions into DXF.
5. Extend the existing canonical document comparison to run equivalent Lua and
   Python fixtures in one test. Compare geometry with tolerances, entity count,
   layers/metadata, and undo/redo outcomes; the current Python-only dump is
   insufficient.
6. Python operation-option toolbar wiring is implemented in the existing
   loader. The `MWindowTest.PythonOperationOptionsWireDefaultAndInitSpecificActions`
   regression exercises default and `_init_`-scoped buttons through the actual
   toolbar against the live Python operation instance. Cross-platform artifact
   validation for that path remains part of the Windows/Snap gates.
7. Exercise all 14 writable DXF variants (seven revisions in ASCII and binary)
   through Python `exportFile`/`importFile`. Assert each variant's stable ID,
   revision tag, encoding metadata and on-disk signature, a complete import
   result, and preservation of one line, circle, and arc. Also assert legacy
   `File.open` returns the `File::Type` corresponding to each stable variant,
   so the compatibility API cannot collapse binary DXF back to ASCII. The test
   uses `boost::filesystem::unique_path` for its temporary DXF artifacts, as
   do the shared persistence and custom-entity test helpers used by the
   cross-platform filters. Those suites must have no POSIX-only process-ID
   dependency or shared temp-path collision. The matrix must fail if a variant
   is missing, unwritable, mislabeled, or cannot round-trip; a non-empty format
   list or a single R2000 smoke is not sufficient.

8. Preserve the R2018+ multiline ATTRIB's `Embedded Object` MText payload,
   including group-3 continuation chunks before the `AcDbAttribute` subclass.
   Import its formatted value and insertion point as MText with the owning
   attribute's layer/metadata/block; retain the explicit limitation that tags
   and attribute linkage are not modeled. The required DXF filter must cover
   the source fixture and a save/reopen round trip on every provider lane.

**Exit:** the 14-variant matrix, persistence, and required parity tests run with
no required-test skips. A dependency blocker remains an explicit unmet release
gate until corrected.

### S6 — Complete every platform's build and packaging sub-plan

Common provider discovery, ABI inventory, runtime layout, and stdlib install
rules live in `lcadpythonscript/CMakeLists.txt`,
`lcadpythonscript/python_runtime_config.h.in`, and the generated config header;
use that selected `Python_EXECUTABLE`/`Python::Python` provider, not an
unrelated shell `python3`. Platform staging/relocation is handled by the
existing install/package scripts. Verify runtime/library/stdlib versions
before copying. Stage provider licenses and dependencies of extension modules
as well as libpython.

| Artifact | Installed runtime | LibreCAD scripts |
|---|---|---|
| Linux bundled | Under AppDir/snap prefix, e.g. `usr/lib/librecad/python/` | `usr/share/librecad/lcUIPy/` |
| Windows | `bin/pythonXY.dll` beside the EXE; stdlib/extensions in `bin/python/` | `share/librecad/lcUIPy/` |
| macOS | `Contents/Frameworks/` matching Python framework/runtime | `Contents/Resources/lcUIPy/` |

Generate these relative locations once and pass resolved locations to S3.
Preserve provider-internal layout where it avoids rewriting Python metadata.

**Selected CPython license — all installed artifacts**

- The current runtime install copies stdlib and dynamic modules but not the
  selected CPython license; Windows CPack's `CPACK_RESOURCE_FILE_LICENSE` is
  LibreCAD's own license and does not satisfy this requirement.
- During configure, resolve `LICENSE.txt`/`LICENSE` from the exact selected
  provider's stdlib or executable root, fail closed if absent, and install it
  as `share/librecad/licenses/python/LICENSE.txt` so AppDir, Snap, Windows, and
  macOS install layouts share one relative location. Preserve the full source
  file contents; do not substitute a license from another Python installation.
- App-bundle assembly copies `share/librecad` wholesale, and the installed
  runtime smoke verifies `../licenses/python/LICENSE.txt` whenever the staged
  Python prefix is expected. Check the installed NSIS/AppDir/Snap and relocated
  `.app` artifacts, not just the build tree. This stages CPython's primary
  license only; notices for non-CPython extension dependencies remain separate
  closure work and must not be implied complete.

**Linux system/developer install**

- Honor normal `CMAKE_INSTALL_PREFIX` and `DESTDIR`. Depend on the discovered
  distribution libpython/stdlib; downstream package metadata must declare them.
- Verify source and installed-prefix imports on x86_64 and native arm64.
  Do not require these system-dependent installs to work without system Python.
- Add a dedicated hosted ARM64 source/install job on GitHub's
  `ubuntu-26.04-arm` runner. Reuse the dependency/build/core-filter/install
  smoke script and upload `build/python-runtime-inventory.txt`; the system
  install smoke uses Xvfb with Qt's xcb plugin, and the script declares Xvfb as
  a dependency. Select `LIBRECAD_BUILD_DIR=build-linux-arm64` in the ARM64 lane
  so a reused checkout cannot consume an x86_64 CMake cache. Do not attempt the
  x86_64-only AppImage or Snap packaging steps in this job. The current
  public runner reference lists this label, but local actionlint 1.7.12 does
  not yet know it; encode only the verified Ubuntu 26.04 labels in the
  repository actionlint config and recheck against the live runner reference.

**Linux AppImage — `scripts/ubuntu-install/{installDependenciesAndBuildRepo,createAppImage}.sh`**

- Stage the selected stdlib, dynamic modules, shared Python, and their runtime
  dependencies; remove manual copying of source-bound `path.py`.
- Use packager closure plus appropriate `$ORIGIN` RPATHs; inspect with
  `readelf`/`ldd`. Stdlib paths and extension-library loading both must work.
- Keep the existing Ubuntu upgrade separate from the compatibility floor:
  build/test on the oldest Linux baseline actually advertised.
- The CI invokes `installDependenciesAndBuildRepo.sh` directly, so keep its
  explicit `#!/usr/bin/env bash` interpreter line; `shellcheck` and `bash -n`
  must pass for the Linux, Snap, and macOS shell scripts.
- Keep the pre-linuxdeploy install smoke as a system-provider/import check; it
  may use the selected system `libpython` and must not claim AppDir closure.
  After linuxdeploy has populated AppDir, run `AppDir/AppRun --python-smoke`
  from outside the source tree with poisoned Python paths and
  `LIBRECAD_REQUIRE_BUNDLED_PYTHON=1`, proving `/proc/self/maps` resolves
  `libpython` from `AppDir/usr`. Then test the produced AppImage separately,
  using extraction mode if FUSE is unavailable and also exercising its real
  launcher in artifact validation. Keep workflow-contract regressions for both
  strict checks and their ordering. Native Linux execution is still required
  because the macOS host cannot exercise `/proc/self/maps` or the real x86_64
  launcher.
- In the final AppImage smoke, also set
  `LIBRECAD_REQUIRE_PYTHON_EXTENSION_CLOSURE=1` and require the loaded
  `libssl`, `libcrypto`, `libsqlite3`, `libffi`, and `libz` paths used by
  `_ssl`, `_sqlite3`, `_ctypes`, and `zlib` to resolve under the AppImage's
  `usr` tree. Do not apply this stricter assertion to the pre-linuxdeploy
  AppDir smoke, where dependency bundling has not run yet.

**Linux Snap — `snapcraft.yaml`, `scripts/ubuntu-install/createSnap.sh`**

- Declare amd64 initially and derive library paths from architecture metadata.
- Install Snapcraft idempotently: refresh an existing snap or install it from
  `latest/stable`, then use the explicit `snapcraft pack` command.
- Select the build SDK explicitly and stage the matching libpython, stdlib, and
  extension dependencies from the same base archive. A staged `python3`
  executable alone is not sufficient proof.
- Resolve from the snap's executable/prefix; isolated startup must not rely on
  an inherited `PYTHONHOME` export.
- Resolve Qt plugins from the staged Snap payload, not Qt's compile-time host
  `/usr` path: set `QT_PLUGIN_PATH` and
  `QT_QPA_PLATFORM_PLUGIN_PATH` under `$SNAP/usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/qt6/plugins`.
  The installed smoke must load the xcb platform plugin and the SVG/image
  plugins used by the UI.
- Launch the installed artifact with `snap run librecad --python-smoke`.
  Preserve separate diagnostics for Snapcraft/provider provisioning failures.
- If the host's apt indexes need an additional architecture view, create it as
  a scoped temporary source: refuse to replace an existing file and remove the
  generated source on every exit path.

**Windows — root CMake, `scripts/windows-install/`, workflow**

- The workflow now provisions official x64 CPython 3.14 and passes its root and
  executable to CMake. The Conan branch honors `WITH_PYTHONSCRIPT`; the SDK and
  runtime must remain the exact same installation. Record the generated ABI
  inventory and keep existing Conan C++ dependencies. Persistence uses the
  recursively checked-out `third_party/libdxfrw` target; do not separately
  clone/build the obsolete external provider.
- CMake now requires 3.21+ for Windows runtime-dependency collection, installs
  LibreCAD shared DLLs beside the EXE, collects their transitive DLLs, stages
  selected CPython root DLLs, and installs Qt deployment DLLs/plugins after
  `windeployqt`. Verify extension-module (`.pyd`) dependencies and provider
  module origins in the actual installed package; no Windows result is proven
  by the macOS source build.
- The Windows build script enables unit tests, checks Python test discovery,
  runs the Python runtime/binding suites, deploys Qt, then invokes CPack once.
  The Conan `VirtualRunEnv` supplies dependency search paths to build/test and
  install-time dependency scanning. Keep path/configuration tests portable.
- CI now installs NSIS into a temporary path containing spaces and Unicode,
  poisons `PYTHONHOME`/`PYTHONPATH`, removes external Qt/Python DLL search paths,
  selects `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen` in the separate installed-smoke step,
  and runs the installed EXE smoke. `WinDeployQt` includes `qoffscreen`, and
  the install copies its Qt DLL/plugin tree with the app. Static contract tests
  guard that selection and staging, but the hosted Windows lane is still
  required to validate CMake resolution, DLL closure, NSIS installation, and runtime.

**macOS — `lcUI/CMakeLists.txt`, `scripts/macos-install/`, native workflow**

- Native arm64 and x86_64 jobs and `scripts/macos-install/createBundle.sh` now
  exist. They use prebuilt, architecture-matched Qt/Python/dependencies and
  separate artifacts; hosted execution on both native runners remains required.
- Install staging copies the selected Python runtime and LibreCAD scripts into
  relocatable prefix locations; bundle assembly deploys Qt, stages non-system
  dylib closure, rewrites load paths, and verifies Mach-O files/signatures.
  Local macOS arm64 install, bundle, relocation, and poisoned-environment smoke
  pass against the pinned `third_party/libdxfrw` provider. The native workflow
  configures that submodule directly and does not build a second external copy.
- Keep the hosted x64/arm64 bundle jobs and no-build-machine/Homebrew load audit
  as release acceptance. PR bundles may be ad-hoc signed; protected releases
  still require Developer ID signing, notarization/stapling, and final-artifact
  smoke.
- Report either architecture's missing hosted validation as incomplete
  coverage, not as a passing universal claim.

### S7 — One actual-artifact smoke path and required CI gates

**Files:** `lcUI/main.cpp`, a small proposed
`lcUI/python/pyruntimesmoke.cpp`, existing unit tests/CMake,
`.github/workflows/main.yml`, install/package scripts.

`librecad --python-smoke` now runs after QApplication/provider setup and before
opening a MainWindow. It uses the same package resolver and plugin loader as
normal GUI startup, and returns nonzero for failed imports, plugin discovery or
registration, document/undo assertions, or persistence round-trip. The loader
reports per-plugin failures while preserving normal startup's continue-on-error
policy. Linux CI invokes the staged install and final AppImage with Qt offscreen;
Windows explicitly selects the staged `offscreen` plugin in its separate
installed-smoke step; macOS uses its packaged native platform plugin without
displaying a window. Remove inherited CI Qt plugin paths so smoke exercises the
installed plugin closure. It must:

1. Import `encodings`, `os`, `json`, `pathlib`, and representative native
   modules (`math`, `zlib`, `ssl`, `sqlite3`, `ctypes`, compression/hash modules).
2. Import `lc`, `lcgui`, and `lcUIPy`; load a context-free shipped plugin (the
   gear operation definition) through the production loader after installing
   process-scoped registration hooks. Check
   the expected operation/plugin registry entry. Give the loader a small load
   status result so its current log-and-continue behavior cannot hide smoke failure.
   UI event/command wiring is tested separately under an owned test MainWindow.
3. Create a document/entity, perform an undo/redo, and run a temporary DXF
   round trip when persistence is enabled.
4. Print concise version/home/path diagnostics; return nonzero on missing
   modules, wrong provider paths, failed registration, or traceback.

Run from an unrelated directory with bogus `PYTHONHOME`/`PYTHONPATH`, without
developer DLL/library path additions. Assert the package's expected app-relative
Python home and module origins; when a staged runtime is present, smoke must fail
if Python falls back to the build host even if all imports succeed. An unrelated
host interpreter must not rescue a broken package.
For AppImage, both extracted AppDir and final launcher must pass in a minimal
post-build container/VM without the matching host Python ABI. For Windows/macOS,
use isolated installed-artifact validation and module/DLL origin checks so the
build SDK cannot rescue a broken bundle.

Promote existing deterministic Python/adapters/EventBus/persistence tests to
required gates, then add only the missing regression cases above. Run selected
suites in bounded subprocesses so global interpreter/hooks cannot contaminate
unrelated UI tests. Fail if expected suites discover zero tests, time out, or
skip a required test. Quarantine individual known flaky cases with a reason.

| CI lane | Required scope |
|---|---|
| Linux primary | Core Python/API tests, real Lua parity, persistence/custom entities, deterministic UI lifecycle |
| Linux feature checks | Python OFF application; persistence OFF binding target only |
| Linux arm64 | Native source/installed-prefix core test and import check |
| Windows x64 | Python-enabled build/core tests and actual NSIS install smoke |
| macOS arm64 + x86_64 | Source/core tests and relocated .app smoke per architecture |
| Artifact jobs | AppImage and Snap smoke; final Windows/macOS artifact smoke |

Cache existing dependency providers and Python inputs by OS/architecture/version.
Keep PR build/tests free of release credentials. Gate release publication on all
required artifact lanes; artifacts uploaded for diagnosis are not release proof.
Export test reports, runtime-smoke output, and artifact/runtime identity with each
required job so the evidence ledger below can reference durable CI results.

## 5. Delivery order, dependencies, and definition of done

| Change | Work and dependencies | Reviewable completion |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | PLAN-SYNC bootstrap, before changes 1–9 | Establish and validate the same-change maintenance loop before implementation batches: maintain this roadmap and gate ledger, run the read-only drift checker and regression fixtures, and report PR synchronization results. | Before the first implementation batch, `--full` and checker tests pass and the complete current candidate set is mapped; at PR handoff, the actual-base check passes for tracked paths. Contributors/reviewers own semantic updates; the checker never writes the plan or promotes gates. Hosted workflow success and required branch protection remain separate PLAN-SYNC acceptance criteria. |
| 1 | S0 + S1 | Capability/runtime inventory; persistence exposure fixed; feature/export checks |
| 2 | S2 | Lossless callbacks; six custom-entity slot tests; Lua remains working |
| 3 | S3 + early S7 smoke | Configurable initialization; relocatable install; accurate exit status |
| 4 | S4 | Owner-scoped execution; operation cleanup and traceback tests |
| 5 | S5, starting dependency investigation early | Unskipped custom-entity DXF and actual Lua/Python parity |
| 6 | Linux S6 + S7 | AppImage/Snap and native install gates |
| 7 | Windows S6 + S7 | Matching SDK/runtime; installed NSIS gate |
| 8 | macOS S6 + S7 | Both native architectures; bundle and release-signing procedure |
| 9 | Documentation and final audit | Accurate support/API table and a maintained example plugin |
| Ongoing | PLAN-SYNC, alongside changes 1–9 | After each meaningful implementation, test, CI, packaging, review, or support result, reconcile affected requirements, gate state, evidence freshness, dependencies, next check, and receipt before continuing. | Each plan edit passes `--full`, checker tests, and complete-candidate path coverage; at PR handoff, the actual-base check covers tracked paths. No result exists only in chat/logs, no path is silently omitted, and failures remain visible. The checker detects drift but never writes semantic updates or promotes gates. PLAN-SYNC is validated only after its hosted PR check passes and branch protection requires it. See section 6 for the operational triggers and failure rules. |

Windows/macOS build preparation can proceed alongside shared work. Each platform
change must consume the shared runtime contract, not duplicate it. Keep the
Python-OFF application check on shared-library/API changes.

Completion means all platform rows pass, the six custom-entity callbacks and DXF
reconstruction work, window ownership/error cleanup are covered, documentation
matches shipped capabilities, and PLAN-SYNC passes. No required persistence/API
test may remain skipped.
No new runtime framework, Python package manager, general binding generator, or
broad CMake rewrite is needed.

## 6. Plan self-updating and validation

### Self-update contract at a glance

This plan stays current through a required, change-triggered loop: implementation,
test, CI, packaging, review, user-scope, or base-branch changes trigger an update
to affected requirements, gate state, dependencies, next acceptance check, and
evidence receipt in the same change. The read-only checker verifies structure,
changed-path and receipt coverage, and freshness of the newest receipt against
the selected comparison base; the PR workflow reports and fails on
synchronization errors. Contributors and reviewers decide semantic impact and
gate acceptance; automation never rewrites the plan or infers completion.

Close each trigger with this transaction before continuing: inventory the
change/result, map it to owning gates, update only affected requirements/state/
evidence/next checks, append a dated receipt, then run the affected checks and
plan validators. A plan/checker/workflow edit or a failed validation is itself
another trigger; record its outcome and unblock action rather than carrying a
stale receipt forward.

The operational self-update triggers and fail-closed validation layers are
required plan sections enforced by `--full` and regression-tested. In addition
to requiring the headings, `--full` checks that this section retains the
minimum contract: same-change reconciliation after each meaningful result or
blocker; `--worktree` inventory of staged, unstaged, and non-ignored untracked
paths; complete-candidate `--paths`; `--base-ref` for the tracked PR diff;
checker regression tests; and branch-protection acceptance. Each required
detail has a regression fixture that removes it and requires validation to
fail. It also requires every row in the self-update trigger table and the
canonical full-plan and checker-test commands, so deleting an event class or
weakening the routine validation cannot silently reduce the loop. Include a
fixture for the rule `newest plan receipt is unchanged from the comparison base`.
If this contract changes, update the prose, checker, fixtures, and receipt in
the same change. This guard protects the maintenance procedure; it does not
validate product behavior or semantic gate decisions.

The plan update is itself part of the loop and is never exempt from validation.
Use a two-pass receipt so its result covers the final file: edit the plan and
append a draft receipt; run `git diff --check`, `--full`, the checker regression
suite, and `--worktree` against the full local candidate inventory; replace the
draft with the exact commands, counts, coverage, and blockers; then rerun those
checks on the final receipt. Any subsequent edit restarts the transaction. Use
repeatable `--exclude-path <repo-relative-output-prefix/>` only for generated
trees not intended for the PR; the checker rejects unmatched exclusions and
reports every applied exclusion. Use `--paths` when validating a deliberately
selected candidate set or external paths not present in the worktree. Once the
PR diff is tracked, the PR workflow must independently pass
`--base-ref <actual-base>`. A structural or path-coverage pass does not validate
product behavior. In path-validation modes, compare the current newest receipt
with the selected baseline (`HEAD` for `--worktree`/`--paths`, actual PR base for
`--base-ref`) and fail if it is unchanged, even when its gate list happens to
cover the candidate. If the baseline predates this plan, require a valid current
receipt. Keep PLAN-SYNC `implemented` until the hosted workflow passes and
branch protection requires it; record every failure or unavailable check with
its next action rather than weakening the gate.

`--worktree` constructs the local candidate inventory as the union of unstaged
paths, staged paths, and non-ignored untracked paths. It uses NUL-delimited Git
output and `--no-renames` so spaces and renames cannot hide paths. Exclude local
generated outputs only when they are not intended PR content, and state the
exact excluded output tree in the receipt; never silently omit an untracked
source, test, workflow, or documentation file.

PLAN-SYNC exit criteria are conjunctive: (1) each meaningful implementation or
evidence change has an affected-gate update and receipt in the same change, with
local `--worktree` coverage for the full candidate set (or an explicit,
reconciled `--paths` set); (2) the PR workflow checks
the actual base, fails on missing plan updates, unmapped paths, omitted gates,
or stale validated evidence, and runs regression fixtures for those failures;
and (3) the hosted check passes and is required by branch protection. Reviewers
still verify semantic impact and evidence provenance. Until all three hold,
PLAN-SYNC is implemented but not validated.

### Maintenance protocol

Every new user turn is a synchronization checkpoint before implementation
resumes. Classify it as a scope/acceptance change, new evidence (including a
pasted build/test/CI log), or no plan impact. Scope changes update the affected
contract, gates, dependencies, and next checks; evidence updates provenance,
freshness, blockers, and the receipt. If the input changes no requirement,
claim, or evidence, leave those rows untouched rather than manufacturing an
update. The checker remains read-only: this same-change reconciliation belongs
to the contributor, with semantic review by a human.

Use one explicit self-update transaction whenever an input changes plan scope,
implementation, tests, CI, packaging, base revision, or evidence: (1) identify
the changed input and owning gates; (2) reconcile only affected requirements,
dependencies, gate state, evidence freshness, and next acceptance checks; (3)
append a dated receipt describing the change and its validation; and (4) validate
the updated plan and complete candidate paths before continuing. A result from
before the latest plan edit is not validation of that edit. Plan validation is
itself part of the receipt; a structural/path pass does not promote product
gates, and any failed or unavailable required check keeps the affected work
incomplete with an explicit next action.

Required closed loop: before each implementation batch, name its owning gates
and next acceptance check; after each meaningful batch, test/CI result, or
blocker, update affected requirements, gate state, dependencies, next action,
and evidence in this same change; then run the structural checker, checker
regression suite, affected product checks, and complete-candidate path check
before continuing. At handoff, reconcile untracked candidates and run the
actual PR-base check for the tracked diff. A batch is not ready to continue or
hand off while its plan receipt or required validation is stale.

If complete-candidate validation reports a mapped gate as stale, keep the last
successful run in the receipt as historical evidence, set that gate back to
`active`, and name the exact revalidation needed. Do not hide candidate paths
or promote the gate merely because a local run passed; reconcile the gate again
after the candidate has been integrated and its current source snapshot has
fresh acceptance evidence.

Treat incoming evidence as a work event too: pasted compiler/test output,
hosted CI results, PR review feedback, and package/artifact smoke reports must
be checked against the tested revision, platform/provider, command, and
discovered/executed/skipped counts before they change a gate claim. Reconcile
their impact and append the receipt before starting the next implementation
batch. If provenance or coverage cannot be established, record the result as
provisional or blocked and name the missing proof; do not promote a gate from
an unverified summary.

Treat this file as the implementation record. Contributors update it when code,
test, CI, or design evidence changes; it is not an unattended scheduler or a bot
authorized to infer semantic completion. Start each work session by comparing
working-tree/PR paths and current CI reports with sub-plans, gate rows, and
evidence, then choose the next unmet acceptance criterion. Before handoff or
opening/updating an implementation PR, update the affected design, gate state,
next action, and evidence in the same change. A plan-only change must also record
its structural validation result below.

Make ownership explicit so the loop remains safe and repeatable:

| Owner | Responsibility | Must not do |
|---|---|---|
| Contributor/agent | Reconcile implementation, tests, CI, user scope changes, and external reports into affected requirements, gate states, stale evidence, next checks, and a dated receipt in the same change. | Leave a result only in chat/terminal output, or promote a gate from an unverified summary. |
| Plan checker | Validate required sections/rows, evidence shape, local links, path coverage, exemptions, receipt coverage, and stale validated gates; report its inputs and findings. | Edit the roadmap, create evidence, choose semantic impact, or mark work complete. |
| PR workflow | Run the checker against the actual base and run its regression suite; publish a concise summary and fail on structural/synchronization errors. | Treat a missing result as a pass or replace product/platform acceptance checks. |
| Reviewer | Confirm scope, gate mapping, evidence provenance/freshness, test selection, and whether the acceptance criteria were actually met. | Accept structural validation as proof of product behavior. |

Each update is one complete transaction: identify the changed inputs and owning
gates; revise only the affected requirements/state/next check; record the exact
validation command and outcome; then verify path coverage. The update is
incomplete if any of these is missing. At resume, use the newest receipt and
ledger as the handoff point, reconcile them with the current worktree/CI, and
replace stale evidence before continuing.

#### Per-batch checkpoint record

Use this compact record at the start and end of every implementation batch; the
gate ledger and newest review-log row together are its durable form:

| Checkpoint | Required record |
|---|---|
| Before work | Owning gate IDs; bounded change; current state (`active`); one concrete acceptance check. |
| After work/result | Exact changed paths; implementation/test/CI result with revision or diff identity, platform/provider, command/filter, discovered/executed/skipped counts; affected gate state and stale downstream evidence; next acceptance check. |
| Before continuing or handing off | Newest receipt includes every minimum and impact-derived gate; complete candidate paths are checked (including untracked files); structural and checker tests pass; failures remain visible with an unblock action. Use `--base-ref` only for the tracked PR diff, after the change is PR-ready. |

Append a receipt for each meaningful result, including failures and blockers;
do not overwrite history or wait until session end. Keep the ledger's next
action aligned with the newest receipt. A prose-only plan edit does not stale
product evidence; a changed implementation input does, and every affected
validated gate must return to `active` or `implemented` until rechecked.

Keep the synchronization loop in-flight, not just at session close: before a
work batch, mark its owning gates active and name the acceptance check; after
each meaningful implementation batch, test, CI result, or new blocker, reconcile
the affected rows and evidence before starting the next batch. The plan is the
resume point if work pauses. Do not leave a completed batch, failure, or new
validation result only in chat, terminal output, or an external CI page.

“Self-updating” means change-triggered, same-change synchronization with a
required automated check; it does not mean a bot guesses whether code works or
promotes a gate. Keep this file as the sole roadmap/ledger. At each milestone,
the contributor updates affected requirements, dependencies, gate state, next
check, and evidence; CI compares the change set to that record and reports
coverage. A reviewer resolves semantic questions and exceptions. Never defer
the update to a later cleanup PR.

#### Operational self-update and validation

Apply the synchronization loop continuously, not just at PR submission:

| Moment | Required update | Required validation |
|---|---|---|
| Resume or start a batch; new user turn | Classify new input as scope/acceptance change, evidence, or no plan impact; reconcile `git status --short`, incoming CI, newest receipt, and affected ledger rows before implementation resumes. | Run `python3 scripts/validate_python_binding_plan.py --full` and `python3 -m unittest discover -s scripts/tests -p 'test_validate_python_binding_plan.py' -v`; run `--worktree` if candidate paths changed. |
| After each meaningful implementation/test/CI result or blocker | Before starting more work, update affected requirements, gate state/evidence freshness, dependencies, next action, and a new receipt. | Rerun `--full`, checker tests, affected product checks, and `--worktree` with only explicitly recorded generated-output exclusions. |
| PR-ready handoff | Ensure every candidate path and result is represented; keep unresolved work as an explicit blocker. | Run `--base-ref <actual-PR-base>` for the tracked diff, plus the plan workflow and relevant workflow lint. Never claim base-ref coverage for untracked files. |

The checker is the automatic drift detector: it derives path-to-gate impact,
rejects missing same-change receipts, unmapped paths, and stale validated gates,
and prints its coverage report. It does not edit plan prose or infer semantic
completion. The author/agent performs the same-change update and the reviewer
confirms its meaning. The PR job must surface the report in its summary and be
required by branch protection before PLAN-SYNC can be `validated`; until then,
local checks prove local validation only.

`--full` also requires the operational table to retain its three synchronization
checkpoints: resume/start, after each meaningful result or blocker, and PR-ready
handoff. Removing any checkpoint is a structural failure, with one regression
fixture per checkpoint; this prevents an apparently complete plan from quietly
falling back to end-of-session-only validation.

#### Self-update triggers

| Trigger | Required same-change plan update | Validation before continuing |
|---|---|---|
| Target branch advances, or work is rebased/ported to another base | Record old/new base SHAs and the path overlap; reconcile changed implementation assumptions, dependencies, platform workflow, and current gate evidence. Keep old receipts as historical and return affected `validated` gates to `active`/`implemented` until checked on the new base. | Re-inventory all status paths (including untracked files); run complete-candidate `--paths`; run affected product checks on the new base; run `--base-ref <actual-PR-base>` when the tracked PR diff is ready. Do not count commit ancestry or a clean merge as product validation. |
| Source, API, build flags, dependency/runtime, runner ABI, source-vs-prebuilt policy, or package layout changes | Map every changed path to gates; update affected sub-plans and downstream platform gates; pin compatible runner/package ABIs; mark evidence stale where its inputs changed; state the next acceptance check. | Run affected implementation checks and policy regression tests (including that missing prebuilts fail instead of building from source); run `--full`, checker tests, and complete-candidate `--worktree`; run `--base-ref <actual-PR-base>` when the tracked PR diff is ready. |
| Test selection, fixtures, or CI workflow changes | Keep acceptance criteria, test names/filters, required jobs, and gate evidence aligned; record discovered and executed counts, including skips. | Confirm selected tests are built and discovered; run the relevant tests and CI/workflow lint, then run the plan checks. |
| A test/CI result, failure, or blocker arrives | Update the owning gate's state, exact evidence, blocker/unblock action, and dependent rows; preserve superseded results in the review log. | Run `--full` and checker tests after the update; re-run changed-path validation if the PR change set also changed. |
| External evidence arrives (pasted logs, hosted CI, review feedback, package/artifact report) | Verify its revision, platform/provider, command, test discovery/counts, and artifact identity where relevant; update affected rows and downstream evidence, and append a receipt before more implementation work. | Treat incomplete or unverifiable evidence as provisional; run the corresponding acceptance check where possible, then run `--full`, checker tests, and complete-candidate path validation. |
| A capability, platform, architecture, or runtime-support decision changes | Update the completion contract, platform matrix, affected sub-plans, delivery dependencies, and all support claims together. | Validate every newly affected platform/API gate against its own acceptance criteria; run plan checks and changed-path validation. |
| A user turn changes scope, priority, acceptance criteria, or supplies evidence | Record the revised decision and rationale, or verify evidence provenance; identify affected gates and downstream evidence, and return any no-longer-supported validated claim to `active` or `implemented` until its revised criteria pass. | Do not silently narrow scope, discard prior evidence, or treat a request/log as implementation proof. If no plan claim changes, leave the ledger unchanged. |
| Plan, checker, checker tests, or plan workflow changes | Update the PLAN-SYNC contract, freshness/path failure fixtures, CI command, gate state, and receipt; do not invalidate unrelated implementation evidence. | Run `--full`, the complete checker suite, `git diff --check`, and `actionlint` for workflow changes; prove a reused baseline receipt fails and a fresh receipt passes; run `--base-ref <actual-base-ref>` for a tracked PR-ready diff. |
| Plan-only edit | Add a synchronization receipt and run the full structural plan check; do not invalidate implementation evidence unless its requirements or inputs changed. | Run `git diff --check`, `--full`, and checker tests; confirm semantic consistency of the edited rows and record the result in the newest receipt. |

#### Gate-state update contract

Update the ledger as part of the work, not as a retrospective summary. Use these
transitions consistently:

| Event | Plan update in the same change | Validation consequence |
|---|---|---|
| Select a batch | Set each owning gate to `active`; write the bounded change and its next acceptance check. | Run the session-start plan checks and inspect all candidate paths, including untracked files. |
| Code or tests are added, but acceptance is incomplete | Keep the gate `active` or set it to `implemented`; record exactly what exists and what remains. | Run relevant build/tests; do not mark runtime, package, or platform proof from compilation alone. |
| An acceptance check passes | Set `validated` only when all gate-specific checks pass on the recorded revision/provider/artifact; record counts, skips, report, and blockers. | Run the required implementation checks, then the structural checker and regression suite after updating the receipt. Package gates also require the tested artifact digest and runtime inventory. |
| A validated input or dependent contract changes | Return every affected validated gate to `active` or `implemented`, retain old evidence as history, and list the revalidation action. | `--base-ref` must reject a changed input whose gate remains `validated`; rerun the affected gate and downstream consumers. |
| Work cannot proceed | Set the gate to `blocked` only with the specific blocker and a concrete unblock action. | Keep unrelated gates and evidence unchanged; rerun plan checks after the ledger update. |

#### Validation layers and fail-closed rule

Treat validation as four separate claims; a pass at one layer cannot stand in
for another:

| Layer | Required check | What it proves / does not prove |
|---|---|---|
| Plan structure | `python3 scripts/validate_python_binding_plan.py --full` | Gate/platform rows, evidence shape, links, and required plan sections are structurally sound; it does not validate code. |
| Checker regression | `python3 -m unittest discover -s scripts/tests -p 'test_validate_python_binding_plan.py' -v` | The synchronization guard rejects malformed plans and missing path/receipt coverage; it does not establish that acceptance tests pass. |
| Change coverage | `--worktree` while work is in progress; `--base-ref <actual PR base>` for the tracked PR diff. `--paths <complete candidate path list>` is available for explicit inventories. | Every intended changed path is mapped, the newest receipt covers its gates and differs from the comparison-base receipt, and exact generated-output exclusions are reported; never claim `--base-ref` covers untracked paths. |
| Product acceptance | Gate-specific build, discovered/executed tests, runtime/package smoke, and applicable workflow lint on the stated provider/platform | Only these checks can support implementation or platform claims. Hosted/artifact claims require the corresponding hosted job and tested artifact identity. |

Any nonzero checker/test/lint result, zero discovered tests, required skip,
missing candidate path, stale dependent evidence, or unavailable required
platform result keeps the owning gate incomplete. Record the exact failure and
next unblock/revalidation action in the same update; do not mask a failure with
a structural pass or promote a gate because CI has not reported yet.

The checker must enforce machine-checkable structure, minimum path mapping,
receipt coverage and comparison-base freshness, and evidence shape. The contributor/reviewer must enforce
semantic consistency: that the stated checks really passed the gate's exit
criteria and exercised the claimed configuration. A green plan check is never
implementation evidence, and a green implementation test is never proof that
the plan was synchronized.

The sync checker is intentionally a guard, not a plan author: it derives changed
paths and minimum gates, checks that the plan and newest receipt were updated
relative to the comparison base, and emits a concise report. The PR job must surface that report alongside its
pass/fail result so missing synchronization and stale evidence are visible in
the review. It must not rewrite prose, infer semantic status, refresh evidence,
or silently accept unmapped paths. Contributors own those decisions; reviewers
resolve ambiguous impact and exact-path exemptions. This is deliberate
self-updating by an enforced contributor/CI loop, not automatic semantic
rewriting of the roadmap.

For each implementation session:

1. Before changing implementation, run `python3 scripts/validate_python_binding_plan.py --full`
   and the plan-checker regression suite. Resolve any structural errors before
   proceeding. Inspect `git status --short` as well: Git's base-ref diff does
   not report untracked files, so classify every untracked candidate PR path
   separately. On a PR-ready, fully tracked change set, also run
   `--base-ref <base-ref>` and fix missing plan synchronization, unmapped paths,
   or stale validated gates before handoff; never treat that check as covering
   untracked files.
2. Inventory changed source, tests, build/package configuration, docs, and CI
   paths; map each to one or more gates using the ownership map below. A path
   spanning gates maps to all of them. Unmapped paths require an explicit
   exemption with rationale, not silent omission.
3. Before implementation, mark affected gates `active` and write the next
   acceptance check. Keep the design, affected sub-plan, ledger, and test/CI
   selection instructions synchronized in the same working change.
4. After each meaningful implementation batch and each test or CI result,
   update gate evidence and freshness before starting the next batch. Record
   exact command/job, tested revision or diff identity, platform/provider,
   result, and remaining blocker. Re-run full plan validation after editing the
   ledger; use changed-path validation whenever the PR change set changes.
   Advance only when that gate's exit criteria pass; configure/build success
   alone cannot certify a different target or runtime behavior.
5. At session end, reconcile every touched path and incoming CI result against
   the ledger, run the applicable plan checks, and list the next unmet
   acceptance check. Resume from this record across sessions. Never remove a
   requirement or promote a gate merely to make the roadmap appear green.

Use this cadence as a pass/fail handoff contract:

| Checkpoint | Required update and validation | Pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| Session start | Run `--full` and checker regression tests; inventory `git status --short` plus incoming CI; name the next acceptance check | Existing structure is valid and every known change/result has an owning gate and next check |
| Before and after each work batch/result | Mark affected gates active; update requirements, evidence freshness, downstream rows, and receipt before continuing; run affected product checks and `--paths` for the complete candidate set | No implementation batch, new result, or blocker is left only in chat/logs; no stale validated claim remains |
| Before handoff | Run `git diff --check`, `--full`, checker regression tests, complete-candidate `--paths`, and `--base-ref <actual-base-ref>` when the tracked PR diff is ready; record the exact next check | No structural errors, unmapped paths, missing receipt gates, or stale validated evidence; validation blockers are explicit |
| Before completion/release | Reconcile all API, implementation, platform, and artifact rows against current evidence | Every required gate passes its own acceptance criteria; PLAN-SYNC and branch-protection status are reported separately |

If work is interrupted, the latest checkpoint is the resume point. Failed or
unavailable validation is recorded as a blocker, not omitted. A result is not
considered handed off until its plan update and the corresponding structural
and change-coverage checks are complete (or the specific validation blocker is
recorded). `--full` validates structure; it does not replace `--base-ref` for
changed-path coverage. The PR workflow is an additional guard, not a substitute
for updating the plan in the implementation change.

#### Changed-path ownership map

Use this coarse map for impact review; the automated minimum path rules below
are what the checker enforces. Add downstream gates when behavior crosses
boundaries. Paths outside both maps are unmapped until assigned or explicitly
exempted.

| Changed path / input | Minimum gates to review |
|---|---|
| Root/component `CMakeLists.txt`, toolchains, export definitions | S1, S2, affected platform gates, PLAN-SYNC |
| `lcscripting/`, `lcadpythonscript/`, `lcadluascript/`, `lcUI/python/` | S1, S2, S3–S5 as applicable; affected platform gates |
| `lcUI/`, `lcUIPy/`, `lcUILua/`, `lcviewernoqt/`, `lckernel/`, `persistence/` | S2–S6 as applicable; affected platform gates |
| `third_party/libdxfrw/` | S5, S6, S7; every affected platform gate |
| `unittest/`, test fixtures, test filters, `.github/workflows/` | Every gate whose acceptance tests or CI evidence change; PLAN-SYNC |
| `.github/actionlint.yaml` | S7, PLAN-SYNC, all platforms whose runner labels it validates |
| `scripts/`, `conanfile.py`, packaging/deployment configuration | S1, S3, S6, S7, applicable platform gate |
| `doxygen/`, `README*`, scripting examples | S0, DOC; platform claims touched by the edit |
| `PYTHON_BINDING_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` | PLAN-SYNC and each gate whose requirement, state, evidence, or claim changes |

Path mapping is a minimum, not a substitute for impact analysis: behavior and
downstream dependencies can add gates not named directly by the changed path.

#### Automated minimum path rules

These rules are the checker's deterministic minimum map. Match repository paths
using the most-specific exact path or prefix; `*` is allowed only as a trailing
prefix wildcard. `ALL_PLATFORMS` expands to every platform gate in the matrix.
The contributor may add gates after impact review but may not narrow a rule's
minimum. Keep this table aligned with the coarser ownership guidance above.

| Exact path or prefix | Minimum gate IDs |
|---|---|
| `PYTHON_BINDING_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` | PLAN-SYNC |
| `scripts/validate_python_binding_plan.py` | PLAN-SYNC |
| `scripts/tests/test_validate_python_binding_plan.py` | PLAN-SYNC |

| `snapcraft.yaml` | S1, S3, S5, S6, S7, SNAP-AMD64 |
| `scripts/ubuntu-install/createSnap.sh` | S1, S3, S5, S6, S7, SNAP-AMD64 |
| `CMakeLists.txt` | S1, S2, S6, S7, ALL_PLATFORMS |
| `lcadpythonscript/` | S1, S2, S3, S4, S5, S6, ALL_PLATFORMS |
| `lcadluascript/` | S1, S2, S4, S5, ALL_PLATFORMS |
| `lcscripting/` | S2, S4, S5, ALL_PLATFORMS |

| `lcUILua/` | S2, S3, S4, S5, S6, S7, ALL_PLATFORMS |

| `README*` | S0, DOC |

#### Explicit path exemptions

Record any deliberately unmapped path here with a concrete rationale. The
checker accepts only exact repository-relative paths; a broad directory
exemption would hide future changes. No exemptions are currently registered.

| Exact path | Rationale | Review date |
|---|---|---|

Every receipt must name all minimum gates from the changed-path map and any
additional gates found during impact review. For a path-only exemption, include
the exact exempt path and point to its rationale in the exemption table. A
missing or outdated receipt is a synchronization failure even when compilation
and tests pass. Keep previous evidence as history when it becomes stale; never
remove an unmet gate to make the report pass.

Keep changes small: this Markdown ledger, existing tests, and CI reports are the
source of truth. Reuse the same gate IDs in PR descriptions and CI summaries.
Avoid a second JSON/database tracker or plan-management framework. CI should
produce a concise plan-sync report listing changed paths, mapped gates, unmapped
paths, and whether the plan changed. CI checks synchronization but never
auto-commits, changes semantic state, or writes evidence. Every implementation PR
updates affected plan rows or explicitly records why no gate, platform claim, or
evidence is affected. Plan-file edits themselves must pass structural checks and
record the result in the review log.

### Work and platform ledgers

States: `planned`, `active`, `implemented`, `validated`, `blocked`.
`implemented` means the change exists but required validation is incomplete.
`validated` requires the stated acceptance criteria and current evidence below.
A blocked entry records its last achieved state and the concrete unblock action.
All implementation entries start planned: reviewing source is not runtime proof.

| Gate | State | Next action / blocker | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| S0 | active | Run the hosted Windows lane, inspect its uploaded CPython inventory, and confirm all recorded provider paths belong to setup-python; obtain hosted Linux inventory and retain hosted macOS x86_64 validation | `scripts/validate_python_provider.py` rejects non-CPython, debug, free-threaded, version/header/library, and target ABI mismatches; its nine fixtures pass. Latest-base macOS arm64 and Ubuntu 26.04 x86_64 emulation configure against their selected providers. Fresh Ubuntu 26.04 ARM64- and x86_64-container guard invocations validate CPython 3.14.4, 64-bit GIL-enabled builds against their configured headers/libraries (`aarch64`: `/usr/include/python3.14`, `/usr/lib/aarch64-linux-gnu/libpython3.14.so`; `x86_64`: `/usr/include/python3.14`, `/usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/libpython3.14.so`). Windows CI now fails closed if its inventory is missing, empty, not CPython 3.14 x64 release ABI, or lacks expected CMake/provider fields; Linux x64 CI requires and prints the non-empty inventory before packaging.
