### Evidence and revalidation rules

For each validated gate, use one compact, reproducible evidence record with
these machine-checkable labels: `date=YYYY-MM-DD`; `revision=<commit or
base+diff identity>`; `environment=<OS, architecture, providers>`;
`command=<exact command or CI job/filter>`; `counts=<discovered/executed/skipped>`;
`skips=none` or `skips=optional:<named tests>`; `result=<pass/fail>`;
`report=<durable report/artifact link>`; and `blocker=<none or remaining issue>`.
Validated gates must have at least one discovered and executed check; discovered
must equal executed plus skipped, and skipped checks must be explicitly optional.
Package gates also record
`artifact_sha256=<digest>` and `runtime=<runtime inventory>`, so source-build
evidence cannot certify a different binary. A gate cannot be `validated` with
missing fields, zero discovered checks, required skips, a failing result, a
remaining blocker, or evidence for a different artifact/provider.
For an uncommitted test run, record its base revision plus the tested code diff
identity; replace provisional proof with committed CI evidence before release.
The plan does not need to contain its own final commit hash.

When code, tests, build flags, runtime/dependency inputs, or package layout change,
mark affected evidence `stale` and return those rows to implemented/active for
revalidation. Preserve the old result as history. Do not invalidate unrelated
gates for prose-only edits, and do not treat an old green run as proof for changed
inputs. Rerun the affected gates and their downstream consumers, not the entire
matrix after every wording change.

### Validate the plan and the implementation separately

Plan validation has two tiers. On every plan edit, run `git diff --check`,
`python3 scripts/validate_python_binding_plan.py --full`, and
`python3 -m unittest discover -s scripts/tests -p 'test_validate_python_binding_plan.py' -v`.
For in-progress candidate coverage, run
`python3 scripts/validate_python_binding_plan.py --worktree`, adding an explicit
`--exclude-path <repo-relative-output-prefix/>` only for a generated tree that
is not intended PR content; record that exact exclusion in the receipt. For PR
change coverage, run
`python3 scripts/validate_python_binding_plan.py --base-ref <base-ref>` against
the PR's actual base ref; do not substitute `--full`, since it does not inspect
the change set. `--base-ref` sees tracked paths only. The `--worktree` mode
automatically unions staged, unstaged, and non-ignored untracked paths using
NUL-delimited, no-renames Git output. The `--paths` mode accepts explicit
repository-relative candidates and applies the same mapping, receipt,
exemption, and stale-evidence checks when an explicit inventory is needed.
The PR workflow runs the authoritative base-diff check and failure fixtures on
every pull request to `main` or `master`.
PLAN-SYNC adds the checker, deterministic tests, and a PR workflow job; until
the tests and workflow pass and branch protection requires the job, the gate is
not validated. The checker accepts a base revision for changed-path analysis
and has a full-plan mode for plan-only edits. Its
report lists changed paths, mapped gates, unmapped paths, whether the plan
changed, and stale evidence requiring review. It verifies local links, unique
required gate IDs, allowed states, all required platform/architecture rows,
minimum evidence fields for `validated` gates, and path-to-gate coverage. A
mapped implementation/test/CI change without a synchronized plan change fails
closed; unmapped paths fail unless an exact path and rationale are explicitly
recorded in the explicit-exemptions table. The checker also requires the newest
review-log receipt to name every minimum gate mapped from the changed paths;
unmapped exemptions must be cited there. Plan-only edits run all structural
checks. Unrelated edits run structural checks and report no affected gates.

Pass/fail fixtures cover duplicate IDs, unknown states, missing platform rows,
stale/missing validated evidence, broken local links, unmapped paths, mapped
changes without a plan update, omitted mapped gate IDs in the receipt, and an
explicitly exempt path. The checker must not invent evidence, promote states,
mark evidence fresh, or rewrite semantic plan content; contributors own those
decisions and CI reports only structure and coverage.

#### PLAN-SYNC exit criteria

Keep the gate `implemented` until all of these are true; only then may it become
`validated`:

1. On each plan edit, `--full`, the checker regression suite, and
   `git diff --check` pass. In-progress worktrees use `--worktree` for the
   complete staged/unstaged/untracked candidate set; on a PR-ready tracked
   change set, the checker also passes against the actual PR base ref.
2. Reconcile `--worktree` output with `git status --short`; every untracked
   candidate PR file must be included or deliberately excluded with rationale.
   Report exact generated-output exclusions. Do not claim `--base-ref` covered
   untracked files.
3. The newest receipt names all automated minimum gates and added impact gates;
   no mapped path is left without a same-change plan update, and every
   unmapped candidate has an exact, reviewed exemption.
4. A human review confirms the edited sub-plans, gate states, next actions,
   test filters, evidence freshness, and platform claims agree with the code
   and validation results. Structural/path validation alone is not semantic
   approval.
5. The hosted PR workflow passes, and repository branch protection requires
   that job. Report these two facts separately; local tests or a green optional
   workflow do not validate the gate.
6. The PR job summary visibly reports changed paths, mapped gates, unmapped
   paths, stale validated evidence, and checker pass/fail; it never edits the
   plan. The checker regression suite verifies this presentation and escapes
   PR-controlled text so filenames cannot inject markup into the summary.

The review log below is the synchronization receipt: each entry names the
affected gate IDs and summarizes the changed requirement/evidence plus the
validation run. The checker compares those IDs with the path map and rejects a
missing plan update or omitted mapped gate. An entry may add gates after impact
review, but may omit a mapped minimum only by referencing an exact exemption
above. Semantic edits to sub-plans, ledger state, next checks, and evidence
remain a contributor/reviewer responsibility; CI must not infer them from
changed lines.

For each affected gate, manually verify consistency between its sub-plan,
ledger, CMake feature flags, test names, and blocking CI jobs. Confirm each
required test is compiled under the documented flags and selected by its CI
filter; zero discovered tests is failure, not green. Label proposed files/jobs
as proposed until they exist. Record contradictions and stale claims before
implementation proceeds. The checker validates structure and change coverage,
not whether the implementation satisfies the plan.

On every implementation milestone, run its listed acceptance tests and update
the corresponding evidence. Missing tests, zero discoveries, required skips,
timeouts, or ignored failures do not pass. A compile pass does not validate a
runtime claim; a source-tree test does not validate a package; an unsigned PR
bundle does not validate protected release signing.

Before release or declaring the roadmap complete, reconcile every required row
with the tested code/artifact/provider, resolve stale evidence and blockers,
confirm all supported platform rows and API capabilities are covered, and update
README/Scripting support claims. Keep any unmet requirement visible and report
the specific next action.

## 7. Reference points

- Existing architecture/API contract: [doxygen/Scripting.md](doxygen/Scripting.md).
- Vendored startup API and program-directory insertion:
  [pybind11/embed.h](third_party/pybind11/include/pybind11/embed.h).
- CPython isolated configuration:
  [Python initialization configuration](https://docs.python.org/3/c-api/init_config.html).
- Official Windows runtime contents and deployment policy:
  [The embeddable package](https://docs.python.org/3/using/windows.html#the-embeddable-package).
- Snapcraft 9.0 core26 support:
  [Release notes](https://ubuntu.com/docs/snapcraft/9/release-notes/snapcraft-9-0/).
